### 13.10 S4b-BL-32, the common repository (before CMP-8), done in code

**What was done** (branch `claude/sleepy-brown-479259`, on `main` at `3b33d37`, where PR #24 was merged): the
`Repository`'s implementation moves to `:shared` commonMain, so the iOS shell (CMP-8) gets the sync, the import and the
undo without a second copy of them.

- **`CommonRepository`** (`:shared` commonMain, `data/CommonRepository.kt`, `open`): what `AndroidRepository` held,
  moved with its KDoc and unchanged in behaviour. The reads and writes, the two-way sync with the S4b-BL-20 reset
  check, the AI calls, the copy's reads, `applyImport` (merge row by row, copy all or nothing) and `undoCopyImport`.
  The platform parts come in through the constructor: the photo folder (a path), `syncSoon` and `apiFor` (the API
  client for an address and key). The clock is `kotlin.time.Clock` and new visit ids `kotlin.uuid.Uuid` (what `:ui`'s
  house form already used).
- **Photo files through kotlinx-io** (`SystemFileSystem`, already on the classpath through Ktor): write, delete,
  size, a streamed upload source and the downloaded bytes. A row's `path` is the full path in the folder, as before
  (the Android folder is still `filesDir/photos`), so existing rows stay valid. Deletes stay best-effort, as
  `File.delete` was: a failure leaves the file and never hides the error that led to the delete. The imported-photo
  check keeps its two halves: the id check (no separator, no `..`), and an existing file at that name must resolve
  into the photo folder.
- **`CopyUndo`** moved to `:shared` (`app.doorprints.export`, the same package) and **`CopyUndoTest`** to commonTest
  (`kotlin.test` in place of JUnit, the same cases), so the undo's rules run on iOS too.
- **`AndroidRepository`** (`:app`) extends `CommonRepository` with the Android folder, `SyncWorker.syncSoon` and
  `Api.client`, and keeps only what has no common form: `addPhoto(houseId, Uri)` (`Bitmap`, `ExifInterface`, the
  Exif strip, docs/09 L6) and `photoDir()` / `photoFile(id)` as `java.io.File` for the exporters.
  `testConnection`, `refreshAiStatus` and the AI calls now use the injected `apiFor` too (the app passes
  `Api::client`, the call they made before).
- **Tests unchanged**, as the ticket asked: `RepositoryTransactionTest` (it calls `discardUncommittedPhotoFiles`
  with `java.io.File`s through a thin `AndroidRepository` overload) and `SyncServerResetTest` run on the new code.

**Verified, and how.** Locally on JDK 21 (Android SDK platform 37.0 installed in the session): `assembleDebug
testDebugUnitTest :shared:testAndroidHostTest :ui:testAndroidHostTest :shared:compileCommonMainKotlinMetadata
:ui:compileCommonMainKotlinMetadata -Proborazzi.test.verify=true`, and the iOS klibs with
`-Pkotlin.native.enableKlibsCrossCompilation=true` (`:shared:compileKotlinIosArm64`, `compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64`,
`compileTestKotlinIosSimulatorArm64`), so kotlinx-io's file calls build for Kotlin/Native. Counts: `:app` 160 JVM test
runs (180 at `18b631e`, less `CopyUndoTest`'s 20), `:shared` 220 (200, plus those 20), `:ui` 110; no failures; the
64 screenshots match (none re-recorded). The first run found one difference, fixed before the commit: the old
`photoFile(id)` created the photo folder, and `SyncServerResetTest` writes a photo through it on a fresh install; the
common `photoPath` creates the folder the same way. A line-by-line diff of the moved body against the old class shows
only the substitutions listed above. CI (Android, Shared-iOS) runs on the push.

**Code review** (one pass, on `59dfb90`, reading kotlinx-io 0.9.1's JVM and native sources): no blocker; stored
paths, truncation, sizes and deletes match the old code on Android. Fixed after it: the KDoc of the imported-photo
check claimed too much (a dangling link at the id's name is not caught, as the old `canonicalPath` check did not
catch it either); a file that vanishes between that check's two calls now counts the photo as skipped instead of
stopping a merge; on iOS two first-run calls racing to create the photo folder no longer fail ("File exists");
kotlinx-io is named in `:shared`'s dependencies (0.9.1, the version Ktor 3.6.0 brings); a server photo id outside
the backup id rule is not downloaded (defence in depth, the server issues UUIDs). Recorded: **S4b-BL-52**, the photo
rows' absolute paths on iOS. The same local checks pass after the fixes (160, 220, 110; screenshots; iOS klibs).

**For CMP-8:** iOS builds a `CommonRepository` with a folder in Application Support, a "sync soon" of its own
(`BGTaskScheduler`, or none at first) and a Darwin-engine client. Photo decoding on iOS is S4b-BL-39's.

### 13.11 CMP-8a, iOS simulator tests and data fixes, done in code

**Owner decisions of 2026-09-29** (asked before the work started): CMP-8 is **three pull requests**, 8a (the tests
and data fixes, no Xcode project), 8b (the iOS app shell) and 8c (the map); if CI's Keychain refuses the smoke build,
it uses **free ad-hoc signing** (still no paid Apple account); the features iOS lacks at first (the Hunt card, camera
and gallery, *Save a copy*, *Import a backup*, the weekly backup) are **hidden**, not shown disabled; and for the map
the **in-app boundary check is the CI gate** (8c), while the visual TC-M-28 stays for a device.

**What was done** (branch `claude/sleepy-brown-479259`, on `main` at `22b43fd`, where PR #28 was merged; one commit
per part, each first built and reviewed on its own branch, then a fix round and a second review of the delta):

- **CI job `ios-sim-tests`** (`shared-ios.yml`, `270c81d`): on `macos-26` with Xcode 26.4.1 pinned
  (`DEVELOPER_DIR`, checked by a first step in both jobs), it picks an available iPhone of the newest iOS runtime,
  passes its UDID to Gradle (`-PiosSimulatorDevice`, read by `android/shared` and `android/ui`'s build scripts) and
  runs `:shared:iosSimulatorArm64Test :ui:iosSimulatorArm64Test`: every commonTest and the new iosTests. It is a job of
  its own, so a failed simulator boot never fails the compile job. The reports are uploaded on failure. Both path
  lists gain `ios/**` and the Android copy of the boundary data (for 8b and 8c).
- **Room on iOS** (S4b-BL-24, `eb0e1ab`): `iosAppDatabase(path)`; the app's file is `Application
  Support/Doorprints/doorprints.db`. `AppDatabaseIosTest`: every table, a rolled-back transaction, the change flow
  (the change after a write is awaited with a real-time timeout) and the version 1 to 2 migration from a file written
  with version 1's SQL (the
  version 1 schema JSON was never committed, S4b-BL-25).
- **Settings and the Keychain** (S4b-BL-26 and -30, `353d05c`): the Keychain calls behind a small seam, so the tests
  run the real `KeychainSecretStore` logic against a fake. `put` calls `SecItemUpdate` and falls back to `SecItemAdd`
  only on `errSecItemNotFound`, both with after-first-unlock, this-device-only access. A locked Keychain
  (`errSecInteractionNotAllowed`) is `SecretUnavailableException` on read and write, never "no key". A settings write
  runs inside `SecretStore.editing` (a no-op on Android), which puts the old key back when the write fails and runs
  `NonCancellable`, so a cancellation cannot leave the two out of step. `KeychainSettingsTest` (13 tests), with a
  real-Keychain round trip that prints `SKIPPED realKeychainRoundTrip` where the simulator gives the test binary no
  Keychain (-34018 or -25291); CI turns that line into a warning.
- **The iOS stand-ins agree with Android** (S4b-BL-33 and -40, `6f221af`): `parseCoordinate` takes only an optional
  sign, ASCII digits and one `.` or `,` separator, on both platforms (before, `toDoubleOrNull` also took exponents, a
  trailing `d` or `f`, `Infinity` and hex, and Kotlin/Native's parser need not agree with the JVM's on those).
  `Formats.coordinate` on iOS rounds halves up like Java's `%.6f` (`sixDecimalsHalfUp` in common code, checked
  against `String.format` on the JVM by `FormatsParityTest`).
  The iOS clock is `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` (keeps counting while the phone sleeps, like `elapsedRealtime`). The iOS app
  language is one of the four shipped languages, English for any other.
- **Photo files by id** (S4b-BL-52, `14d8160`): `CommonRepository.photoFileOf(id)`, which creates nothing, replaces
  the row's stored `path` wherever the file is read or deleted, so an iOS update that moves the app's container
  cannot strand the photos. `PhotoStalePathSyncTest` proves the upload, the sync tombstone and undoing a copy work from
  a stale path; the rows and the Android folder are unchanged.
- **One backup-excluded data folder** (new S4b-BL-56, `eb0e1ab`, `42ea8fc`): the database and the settings file both
  live in `Application Support/Doorprints`, created with `NSURLIsExcludedFromBackupKey` set and read back
  (`IosDataDirectoryTest`). No iOS build has shipped, so nothing moves.

**Differences from the plan:** none in scope. The user-visible change on Android is the coordinate field: a
coordinate typed as an exponent or a hex number now shows the field's existing range hint (for example "Between −90 and
90", in all four languages) instead of being read. UX pass: nobody types those on the field's decimal keyboard, and every
plain decimal is read as before.

**How it was verified:** on Linux, the iOS klibs and test klibs of both modules cross-compiled
(`-Pkotlin.native.enableKlibsCrossCompilation=true`), and the full Android command of `CLAUDE.md` (the 64 screenshots
unchanged). The first `ios-sim-tests` run (PR #29, `fd960cd`) passed all 237 `:shared` tests, the
real-Keychain round trip skipped (status -25291) as designed, and 113 of 114 `:ui` tests: `ClockIosTest` had assumed
the monotonic clock is never behind `systemUptime`, but each counts from its own start (0.4 s apart on the VM), so
that test was removed. Two review notes are
left open as **S4b-BL-57**.

### 13.12 CMP-8b, the iOS app shell, done in code

**What was done** (branch `claude/sleepy-brown-479259`, on `main` at `a14e57c`, where PR #29 was merged; built as
three parts in parallel on one fixed contract, `4daf304`, then combined): the iPhone app runs the common Compose UI
with the features it has, and CI builds it ad-hoc signed and launches it on a simulator.

- **`PlatformFeatures`** (`:ui` commonMain, `4daf304`, `bee3318`): which features a platform has, all on by default,
  so Android provides nothing and is unchanged (its 64 screenshots byte-identical). The iPhone gets
  `PlatformFeatures.Ios` (owner, 2026-09-29: hide, not disable): the app opens on the Houses tab, and the Map tab
  (kept in the bar) shows its title and "The map isn’t on iPhone yet" with *Open Houses*; Hunt mode and its settings,
  adding photos, *Save a copy*, *Import a backup*, the weekly backup and every way to the map (*Add a house on the
  map* on the empty list and Compare, the Assistant's *Go to the map*) leave no entry point; the empty list says houses
  cannot be added on iPhone yet and that a connected server brings them, with *Open Settings*; the language section
  says how to give Doorprints its own language in the Settings app and opens it; the privacy note names the iPhone and
  leaves out the map tiles and the geocoder it does not use. Five new strings in four languages (hi, ta, te *under
  review*); 48 new screenshots (`ios_*`: the Map tab, Settings, the empty list, the house form, Compare's empty state,
  the Assistant when off; four languages, both themes); `PlatformFeaturesTest`.
- **The Kotlin side** (`1004774`): the static framework `DoorprintsKit` from `:ui` (both iOS targets;
  `embedAndSignAppleFrameworkForXcode` for Xcode). `MainViewController()` renders `DoorprintsRoot` in
  `ComposeUIViewController` with the iOS services: `IosAppContainer` (one per process: `CommonRepository` on the
  CMP-8a database and settings, photos in the backup-excluded data folder, one Ktor Darwin client, `IosApiHttp`, 90 s
  per request, App Transport Security at its default; every job in the app's scope catches its own failures but
  lets cancellation through; one sync at launch and one 3 s after edits settle), `IosPlatformServices` (VoiceOver,
  the location state from one shared `CLLocationManager`, *Open Settings*, the dialler, links over http(s) only), a
  one-shot `CLLocationManager` fix for *Plan visits* and the real location prompt, and the swipe-back through `NavigationBackHandler` (navigationevent-compose 1.1.0, the version navigation
  2.9.2 already resolves). What iOS hides gets inert services. A Debug build launched with `-DoorprintsSelfCheck`
  prints `DOORPRINTS-SELFCHECK <resources|database|settings|keychain> PASS|FAIL|SKIP` and a final `done` line (once
  per process), never a value.
- **`ios/` and CI** (`2eea7d9`): an XcodeGen 2.46.0 spec (`project.yml`: app `Doorprints`, bundle `app.doorprints`,
  iOS 17, iPhone, the Gradle pre-build script that finds JDK 21, arm64 simulator), a SwiftUI app of a few lines
  around `MainViewController()`, a hand-written `Info.plist` (`CADisableMinimumFrameDurationOnPhone`, the location
  and local network purpose strings in four `.lproj` folders, no ATS exceptions), `ios/ci/launch-smoke.sh` and
  `ios/README.md`. New job
  **`ios-app`** in `shared-ios.yml` (macos-26, Xcode 26.4.1, 60 min, `contents: read`): XcodeGen checked with
  `shasum -a 256 -c`, the project generated and `plutil -lint`ed, `xcodebuild build` for the simulator ad-hoc signed
  (`CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY=- CODE_SIGNING_REQUIRED=NO`, `CODE_SIGN_STYLE: Manual` in `project.yml`; owner-approved, free,
  no identity or Apple account; the signature gives the app its Keychain), the bundle checked for
  `compose-resources`, the plist keys and the signature, then the launch smoke: boot an iPhone of the pinned SDK's
  runtime, install, launch with the self-check, require `done PASS` (from the console, or from the simulator's
  unified log when the console has none), take a screenshot; the logs, the screenshot and the build log are
  uploaded. Each of the workflow's three jobs has its own `~/.konan` cache key.
- **Also:** the CMP-8a note S4b-BL-57 (1) fixed (both receives in `AppDatabaseIosTest` under a real-time timeout);
  the "compile-only" wording in the module READMEs and KDoc brought up to date.

**Review.** Code review: no must-fix; fixed: the launch smoke also reads the unified log; the ATS wording (a LAN IP
address or `.local` name is not covered, so `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` was added); ad-hoc signing so the
simulator app has a Keychain; `openUrl` without the `canOpenURL` gate; the self-check started once per process;
cancellation rethrown by the app scope's jobs (no `runCatching`); one shared `CLLocationManager` for status reads and
the KDoc on threads; a remembered notification-request lambda; the redundant `.ignoresSafeArea(.keyboard)` dropped;
the target's duplicate deployment target dropped and a `~/.konan` cache key per job. Design review: fixed: the start
destination and the tab pops follow the platform (Houses on iPhone, the Map tab kept); the iPhone's own privacy note;
no *Add a house on the map* or *Go to the map* on iPhone, and the empty list explains and offers *Open Settings*; the
language sentence in the Server intro's text style, reworded for iOS's per-app Language row, with *Open iPhone
Settings* as a `ButtonLabel`; the Map tab's title, with the note below it; a typographic apostrophe and ‘Houses’
quoted in all four languages; the map's add tip is never set on iPhone.

**First green launch (PR #30, `7dc7d48`, 2026-09-29):** the `ios-app` job built the app, ad-hoc signed it, installed
it on an iPhone simulator (iOS 26.4) and launched it: `DOORPRINTS-STARTUP` database, settings, repository, ready, then
`DOORPRINTS-SELFCHECK` resources, database, settings and **keychain PASS** (so ad-hoc signing gives the app a
Keychain) and `done PASS`; the screenshot shows the Houses tab with the iPhone empty state. Four earlier runs had
crashed at launch: the crash report showed SIGSEGV inside `NSLog("%@", text)`, a Kotlin `String` passed through C
varargs, which Kotlin/Native does not turn into an `NSString`. All logging now goes through `logLine()`
(`IosLog.kt`), which passes the text as the format string. On the way, the job gained an uncaught-exception hook
(`DOORPRINTS-CRASH`), start-up breadcrumbs, a detached launch read through the unified log, and collection of crash
reports and the system log. Also in this pull request: the backend's Jackson 3 and Jackson 2 overrides for
CVE-2026-68497 (the Trivy SBOM gate, [07](07-secure-build-and-deploy.md) v0.43).

**How it was verified:** on Linux, the full Android command of `CLAUDE.md` (the 64 Android screenshots unchanged, the
48 `ios_*` ones recorded), the iOS klibs and test klibs of `:shared` and `:ui` cross-compiled, `:ui:tasks` lists
`embedAndSignAppleFrameworkForXcode` and the framework links, YAML and plist parses, `bash -n` and shellcheck on the
launch smoke, which was also run against a stand-in `xcrun` for a pass on the console, a pass found only in the
unified log (with the app still running and after it ended), a failure and a crash. Linking the framework, building
the app and launching it run for the first time in CI on this branch. Left for later: S4b-BL-58 and -59.

### 13.13 CMP-8c, the iPhone map, done in code

**What was done** (branch `claude/sleepy-brown-479259`, on `main` at `64ceabd`): the iPhone app has the map, with India's
boundary as the Government of India shows it (ADR-22), and adding a house on it (S4b-BL-58).

- **The India rules on a style held as JSON** (`:ui` commonMain). The plan (item 18) was to apply them on iOS through
  a Swift bridge over MapLibre iOS's layer API, whose filters are `NSPredicate`s: every rule would have been converted
  from MapLibre's JSON and back, and `within` and `zoom` in a filter may not survive that (the plan's open risk). The
  plan's fallback is used instead, as the main path: `JsonStyleOps`, a `StyleOps` over the style's JSON, runs the same
  `applyIndiaView` steps as Android (filters ANDed with `all` as JSON, the outline's data inline), then
  `prepareMapStyle` adds the houses' source and layers on top, and MapLibre iOS gets the whole style as JSON
  (`MLNMapView.styleJSON`, confirmed in the 6.31.0 headers). Swift never builds or changes a filter.
  `MAP_STYLE_URL` and `housesGeoJson` moved to common code (`MapStyleJson.kt`, kotlinx.serialization instead of
  org.json; Android draws the same houses).
- **The in-app boundary check** (`IndiaViewCheck`, the owner's CI gate of 2026-09-29): on the prepared JSON, the
  disputed lines are in the style and hidden; `boundary_2` starts at zoom 5 and holds the country-line rule and the
  tile-zoom guard; `boundary_3` holds the guard and the held areas' `within` rule, and every other boundary line
  layer from zoom 5 the guard; the outline's source has its world, claim and state lines, `in-boundary-world` (below
  zoom 5) sits directly above `boundary_2`, `in-boundary-claim` directly above it, `in-boundary-state` (from zoom 5)
  directly above `boundary_3`, each with its filter; every state label layer holds the state-label rule; the house
  layers are the top two. It reads the result, not the steps, so a step skipped with a warning (rule 5) still fails it,
  and a Liberty change that renames a layer fails it too. `loadedProblems` compares what `MLNMapView` reports it loaded
  with the JSON it was given (MapLibre drops a layer it cannot read with only a log line). `JsonStyleOpsTest` (11
  tests, TC-U-81) on an excerpt of the real Liberty style; by hand, the full Liberty style of 2026-09-29 prepared the
  same way passed the check and MapLibre's style-spec validator (0 errors).
- **iOS** (`iosMain`, `ios/`): `IosMap.kt`, the seam (Kotlin interfaces the Swift app implements: text and numbers
  only); `IosMapStyle` (downloads Liberty, two tries of up to 20 s, keeps the last good copy in Caches for an offline start,
  reads the geo files from the bundle); `PlatformMap.ios.kt` (`UIKitView`, touches straight to the map, VoiceOver
  into MapLibre's own elements); `ios/Doorprints/MapLibreMapView.swift` (`MLNMapView`: north-up, no logo, the
  attribution where the chrome says, camera, houses, label size, the location dot, a tap within 24 pt picks the
  nearest house, a long press adds one). MapLibre iOS 6.31.0 comes from MapLibre's Swift package, pinned by revision
  `13e41ab3d77ff5113e7e5d4ee87803b1f81f5683` in `project.yml` (its binary target pins the xcframework's SHA-256);
  `android/app/src/main/assets/geo` is bundled as `geo/`, so there is no third copy of the boundary data.
- **The gate in CI** (TC-I-38): the self-check gains `indiaView` (prepare the style as the map does; the check finds
  nothing) and `map` (the map on screen loaded all of it, up to 90 s); `launch-smoke.sh` waits up to 300 s and passes
  only with both lines PASS; `ios-app` caches the Swift package checkouts and checks that `MapLibre.framework` is
  embedded, that `Package.resolved` holds the pinned revision and that both geo files match the repository's.
- **The iPhone's features**: `PlatformFeatures.Ios` now has the map, so the app starts on the Map, *Add a house on
  the map* and *Go to the map* are back, and the empty list is Android's. Hunt mode stays off: the Map shows no Hunt
  card, only the location note, whose texts (`map_location_off_no_hunt`, `map_needs_precise_no_hunt`, four languages,
  hi/ta/te *under review*) name only *Save house here*. The iPhone's privacy note gains the map tiles (four
  languages); it still leaves out the geocoder and photos, which iOS has not got. 38 `ios_*` screenshots re-recorded
  (the Map's chrome, rendered with the map view as an empty box, since MapLibre cannot run under Robolectric; the
  empty list, Compare's and the Assistant's ways to the map; Settings); Android's 64 unchanged.

**Review.** Code review (one pass, before the first CI run): **blocking, fixed:** `MLNMapView(frame:)` loads
MapLibre's demo style (`MLNMapView.mm`: `[MLNStyle defaultStyleURL]`), whose borders ignore ADR-22, until Liberty
replaces it, and its load (or, offline, its failure) was taken as the map's own; the view now starts on an empty style
and Kotlin counts only the load or failure of the style it handed over. Should-fix, fixed: the smoke's wait (300 s,
the checks run one after another), `indiaView`'s own 60 s limit, one retry of the style's download, the view released
when it leaves the screen (`shutDown()`: no location updates, no callbacks; first named `release()`, which the Swift build refused because `NSObject` has one). Nits, fixed: `compassVisibility`, a
negative direction to keep the bearing, the density read when it is used. No problem found in the Swift names Kotlin
exports, the MapLibre 6.31.0 calls, the XcodeGen spec or `JsonStyleOps`' equivalence with `MapLibreStyleOps`.

**First green run (`61e1b40`, 2026-09-29, Shared iOS compile run 36539902921):** the Swift map compiled against
MapLibre iOS 6.31.0 from its package (the one earlier failure: a `release()` that clashed with `NSObject`'s, renamed);
the bundle held `MapLibre.framework`, the pinned revision and both geo files unchanged; on an iPhone Air simulator
(iOS 26.4) the self-check printed resources, database, settings, keychain, **indiaView and map PASS** and `done PASS`.
The launch screenshot shows the Liberty map of India at the start camera with the legend, the attribution button,
the zoom and location buttons and *Save house here*, and no Hunt card; it replaces the user guide's iPhone pictures
(`guide/docs/images/iphone-map.png`; the old "no map yet" launch shot removed, the guide's table and iPhone section
updated). Most of the smoke's 7 minutes were the simulator's first boot ("Waiting on Data Migration"). The next run (`9c96efb`, guide and docs only) failed the gate with every check passing: `log stream` dropped the `indiaView PASS` line while keeping the lines around it, and the smoke read the stream alone. It now reads the stream and a fresh `log show` together.

**How it was verified:** on Linux, the full Android command of `CLAUDE.md`; the iOS Kotlin cross-compiled
(`:ui:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` with `-Pkotlin.native.enableKlibsCrossCompilation=true`, which caught one
cinterop mistake before CI); every MapLibre iOS name the Swift file uses read in the 6.31.0 headers. The Swift file,
the package, the app build, the launch and the gate run for the first time in CI on this branch. **Not yet:** TC-M-28,
the owner's look at the map on an iPhone.

### 13.14 S4b-BL-69, Hunt mode on iPhone, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-bl-69-ios-hunt-mode`, from `main` at `fd746b4`): the iPhone app has Hunt mode, from
the same engine as Android (`HuntEngine`, §13 0.80), with the path trace of S4b-FR-2 inside it. Owner request of
2026-09-29: "add Hunt mode to iOS as well so that we have less to develop later".

- **`IosHunt`** (`:ui` iosMain), the adapter, what `HuntService` is on Android: one `CLLocationManager` per run
  (`kCLLocationAccuracyBest`, every fix, `CLActivityTypeFitness`, automatic pausing off, background updates on under
  the *When in use* permission with the blue indicator shown; `UIBackgroundModes` `location` in `Info.plist`, checked
  in the app bundle by CI), a delegate on the main thread, the engine in a main-dispatcher scope that ends with Hunt
  mode. Core Location has no request interval, so `HuntFixThrottle` hands the engine fixes at the fused client's rate
  (walking: every 15 s, or 5 m after 5 s; staying: every 60 s, or 10 m after 30 s), which keeps the Hunt card and the
  stay detector fed while standing still. The battery from `UIDevice` (monitoring on while Hunt mode runs; null in the
  simulator, so no stop there). A revoked permission or a denial error ends Hunt mode with `NO_PERMISSION`, as on
  Android; `NOT_ALLOWED` is Android-only. The alerts are worded here from the Compose resources (the `notif_*` strings
  now in both places with the same text, `StringParityTest`; `hunt_battery_text` says "percent", Compose resources
  showing a bare `%` as written) and posted from a scope of their own, so the low-battery alert survives the stop that
  follows it.
- **`IosNotifications`**: `UNUserNotificationCenter` with a delegate set from the Swift app's `init`
  (`MainViewControllerKt.installNotifications()`, before the app finishes launching, as a tap that starts the app
  needs); banners with sound in the foreground too; the prompt (alerts and sound, no badge) asked in context when Hunt
  mode is turned on, through the common `rememberNotificationAsk`; the authorization read asynchronously and kept, so
  the synchronous `canPostNotifications` and the Hunt card's *Allow notifications* (the prompt while iOS will still
  show it, else the Settings app) answer from the last read. A tapped alert becomes a `DeepLink` (`notificationDeepLink`:
  a UUID for the house or visit, coordinates in range, as MainActivity checks its intent, F-25); the "are you at a
  house?" alert comes down once its visit is saved as a house (`clearVisitAlert`).
- **`IosGeocoder`**: Apple's `CLGeocoder` (free, no key) for the street alerts and, from the same object, the new-house
  form's address (`reverseGeocode`, which returned null on iPhone until now); 10 s timeout, cancelled with the caller,
  main thread. The iPhone privacy note names it, as Android's names Google's geocoder.
- **`PlatformFeatures.Ios.huntMode = true`**: the Hunt card on the Map, the Hunt mode section in Settings (the sliders
  and *Trace my path on the map*) and the location note's Hunt wording are the common screens'; `ios_map` and
  `ios_settings` re-recorded (16 images). The location purpose string names Hunt mode in the four languages
  (`Info.plist`, `<lang>.lproj/InfoPlist.strings`).
- **CI** (`shared-ios.yml`, `ios/ci/launch-smoke.sh`, TC-I-41): the bundle check requires the background mode; the
  smoke grants the app location (`simctl privacy`), sets a simulated location about 15 m from where the self-check
  saves a house (`simctl location set`, cleared at the end) and requires `DOORPRINTS-SELFCHECK hunt PASS` alongside
  `indiaView` and `map`: the check saves the house, starts `IosHunt`, waits until the engine names it as the nearest
  house from a fix, stops and deletes the house (SKIP without the permission, FAIL when the start is refused or no fix
  names it within 60 s). Notifications cannot be granted on a simulator, so iOS drops the alerts; the engine's state
  is what is checked. `IosHuntTest` (iosTest, 3 tests): the throttle's two rates and the deep-link check.

**Decisions.** *When in use* with background updates, never *Always* (PRV-001, PRV-024: Hunt mode runs from the visible
app; iOS shows the blue indicator while it collects in the background). No ongoing notification as on Android: the
indicator is iOS's own sign that the app uses location. What the lock screen shows is iOS's *Show Previews* setting
(F-14 on Android is `VISIBILITY_PRIVATE`). Hunt mode's alerts are the iPhone app's only notifications.

**How it was verified:** on Linux, `tools/check.sh android ios guide licence` (the Android command with the screenshot
verify, `StringParityTest` over the shared keys, the iOS main and test klibs cross-compiled, which caught two
cinterop names: `CLActivityType` and `UNAuthorizationStatus` are integer typealiases, not enums). The adapter, the
notifications, the simulated location and the new gate ran for the first time in CI on this branch: the first run
(`e9949ad`) timed out in `hunt` with every other check passing, since a fixed simulated location is delivered once,
at the start of updates, before the engine's house list had loaded; the smoke now steps the location about 11 m every
10 s while it waits, and the adapter leaves `DOORPRINTS-HUNT` breadcrumbs (the start, each fix's accuracy, an error's
code, the stop; debug binaries only) in the smoke's artifact. **Not yet:**
TC-M-31, the owner's walk with an iPhone (background execution with the screen off, the alerts' timing, the battery
stop, the trace's line).

### 13.15 S4b-FR-6, offline maps, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-fr-6-offline-maps`, from `main` at `f2a3fc4`): the third step of the map work
(N13 4a, after the path trace and Hunt mode on iPhone): a saved area of the map that draws with no network, on both
phones, from MapLibre's own offline packs.

- **Common** (`:ui` commonMain, `OfflineMaps.kt`): `GeoBounds`, `OfflineTiles` (Web Mercator tile counts from zoom 0
  to 14, 50 KB a tile for the estimate, `MAX_TILES` 2,000), `OfflineArea` with its state (saving with the bytes so
  far, ready with its size, failed) and `OfflineMapsServices`, one small interface each phone implements; `NoOfflineMaps`
  for previews and tests (no button, no section). `MapControl.visibleBounds()` gives the box on screen (Android:
  the projection's visible region; iPhone: `visibleCoordinateBounds` through four new `IosMapView` calls).
- **UI** (`OfflineMapsUi.kt`): the Map's fifth control, a download glyph built from its path like the minus, *Save
  this area for offline*; `SaveAreaDialog` (the estimate, the mobile-data note, the name from the geocoder's locality
  within the location timeout or "My area", the refusal over the cap with the numbers); the snackbar when the save
  starts and again when the area is ready (with its size) or failed; Settings > Offline maps with the hint (what
  saved areas do; the OpenStreetMap and OpenFreeMap credit), the list with size or progress and *Delete* after a
  confirmation, or "No areas saved yet". Strings in four languages (hi, ta, te *under review*).
- **Android** (`AndroidOfflineMaps` in `:app`): `OfflineManager.createOfflineRegion` with an
  `OfflineTilePyramidRegionDefinition` (the Liberty style, the box, zoom 0 to 14, the screen density), the id and
  name as JSON metadata, an observer for the progress, the regions listed once per process and an incomplete one
  set active again (a download the process died in carries on), delete through the region. The metered state from
  `NetworkState`.
- **iPhone** (`IosOfflineMapsServices` over `IosOfflineMaps`, implemented by `MapLibreOfflineMaps.swift`):
  `MLNOfflineStorage.shared.addPack` with an `MLNTilePyramidOfflineRegion`, the id and name as the pack's context,
  KVO on `packs` for the load, the progress and error notifications, every change reported to Kotlin as one snapshot;
  `NWPathMonitor` for the metered state; registered from the Swift app's `init` beside the map factory. The packs
  fetch the Liberty style's tiles, the same addresses the map's inline style asks for, so the store serves both.
- **Docs and tests**: [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.20 built, [03](03-design.md) §11.2 (OpenFreeMap's
  terms and the cap) and the component table, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-95 and TC-M-32, [01](01-requirements.md)
  NFR-004, the guide's map page, `ios/README.md`; `OfflineTilesTest` (4), the `offline_maps` screenshots (8 new
  images); `settings`, `ios_settings` and `ios_map` unchanged, since the screenshot app initialises no MapLibre and
  the store then reports itself unsupported (no section, no control), as in a preview.

**Decisions.** The area is the box on screen, not a drawn one or a hunting area (5.17 comes with 4c); the person
decides about mobile data each time (a warning, no setting); the cap is 2,000 tiles an area with the numbers shown
when refused; no ongoing notification while a download runs (the Settings list has the progress, and MapLibre keeps
downloading while the app is open or in the background as the platform allows). OpenFreeMap's public tiles remain the
source within that cap, and the owner's reading of its terms is asked for ([14](14-lead-backlog-and-handoff.md) §6);
the website's offline tiles are their own change (S4b-BL-79).

**How it was verified:** on Linux, `tools/check.sh android ios guide licence` (the Android command with the
screenshot verify and the new shots; `StringParityTest` and `PlatformFeaturesTest`; the iOS main and test klibs
cross-compiled). The Swift store, `MLNOfflineStorage` and the packs run for the first time in CI on this branch (the
app build and the launch smoke, which does not save an area). **Not yet:** TC-M-32, the owner's saved area on a
phone and the boundary re-check offline.

### 13.16 S4b-FR-3, sharing updates on Android, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-fr-3-share-updates-android`, from `main` at `445f021`, the design of
[11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.28 and ADR-27 built as designed):

- **`:shared`**: `ExportOptions.since` and `sharedTo` (`isUpdate`); `ExportBundle.build` keeps, with `since`, the
  houses and visits with `updatedAt` after it and the photos with `createdAt` after it, every visit and photo of a
  changed house, and the unchanged house of a changed visit (so the visit has somewhere to land); the file stem
  `Doorprints-updates-<date>`; `BackupManifest.sharedSince` and `sharedTo`, optional and ignored by every reader
  (docs/schemas 1.7). `ShareContact` (id, name, `lastSharedAt`) as JSON in the settings store, on the phone only:
  `addShareContact` (trimmed, one per name in any case), `markShared`, `removeShareContact`; the redacted print
  says only how many.
- **`:ui`**: `ShareUpdatesScreen` (Settings > Your data > *Share updates with…*): the names, *Add a name*, what the
  chosen person last got and when, *Include contact details* with the copy's warning, *Include photos*, the counts
  from the rows through the same bundle, *Share updates with Priya*; the export worker writes the ZIP into the
  share-copies folder (the Export screen's stop, cleanup and notification apply), the finished run's file goes to
  the share sheet and `lastSharedAt` moves to the export's instant once the sheet opened (Android does not report
  whether a file was sent). `DeepLink.ImportFile` and the Import screen's `initialFile`, picked once; its header
  reads "Updates for Priya, made on <date>" from the manifest. Strings in four languages (hi, ta, te *under
  review*).
- **`:app`**: `ExportRequest` carries `since` and `sharedTo` to the worker, `Exporters` writes them into the
  manifest; MainActivity turns an `ACTION_VIEW` of a `content` or `file` document, or an `ACTION_SEND` with a
  stream, into `DeepLink.ImportFile` (a link is never taken; F-25: the Import screen validates the file as any
  picked one); the manifest's intent filters for `application/zip` and `application/json`.
- **Tests and docs**: `ShareUpdatesTest` (4), `SettingsStoreTest` (+1), the `share_updates` screenshots (8, and
  `settings` re-recorded for its new row);
  [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-96, TC-M-33; [01](01-requirements.md) PRV-029; [02](02-threat-model.md) T-I31;
  docs/schemas 1.7; the guide's data page; CHANGELOG.

**Not built here:** the web (S4b-BL-75 first) and the iPhone (S4b-BL-81 first); deletions (S4b-BL-82); the Drive
folder (with sign-in, D-28). **Not yet:** TC-M-33, the owner's exchange between two phones.

### 13.17 S4b-FR-4, a shared listing, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-fr-4-listing-share`, the design of [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md)
5.29 built as designed, with 5.9's rule kept: the listing page is never fetched):

- **The no-AI parser**, twice, over one fixture file: `ListingText` (`:shared`, `shared/listing/ListingText.kt`) and
  `parseListingText` (`web/src/app/shared/listing-text.ts`), with `docs/schemas/listing-fixtures.json` (seven
  hand-written share texts in the portals' shapes; `ListingFixturesTest` and `listing-text.spec.ts` read every case
  field by field). The label from the first line that is not the link; the price with ₹, Rs or INR and k, lakh or
  crore, or a bare lakh or crore amount; RENT from rent words, SALE from sale words or ten lakh and above; BHK or a
  studio; the locality before a city name (a list of Indian cities); the area and the furnishing at the top of the
  notes, the whole text under them; the first link with `utm_*`, `fbclid`, `gclid`, `igshid`, `ref` and `src`
  removed and the portal from a host allowlist matched by suffix; a phone number only from the text. On Android the
  raw fields go through `DraftSanitizer` as the model's do, so the caps and the "in the text" rules apply; the web
  port carries the same caps.
- **Android**: MainActivity takes `ACTION_SEND` `text/plain` (the subject first when it is not in the text; 20,000
  characters at most, SEC-043) as `DeepLink.NewHouseFromListing`; the manifest's `text/plain` filter puts
  Doorprints in the portals' share sheets. `DoorprintsRoot` looks the cleaned link up in the houses: a match asks
  "You saved this listing on <date>" with *Open* and *Add anyway*; otherwise the Map opens with the tip "Where is
  this house?" (`map_add_tip_listing`, and its screen-reader form) and, once a place is chosen, the new-house form
  takes the text (`listingText`, consumed once) through `mergeListing` and the same summary as *Fill in from listing
  text*. Strings in four languages (hi, ta, te *under review*).
- **Web**: the new-house page runs the parser when the share page's text arrives (`openDraft`), so the fields fill
  on arrival; *Fill in from listing text* (AI) stays the second pass.
- **Tests and docs**: `ListingTextTest` (4), the two fixture tests; [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-97, TC-M-34;
  docs/schemas README (the fixture file); the guide's houses page; CHANGELOG.

**Not built here:** the label-within-100 m duplicate (the link check is), the locality lookup (S4b-BL-83), brokers
(with 4c), the hollow "approximate" marker (FR-068, with 4c). **Not yet:** TC-M-34.

### 13.18 The records foundations, slice 0 of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-0-records`; the design of [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.30
and [03](03-design.md) ADR-28, slice 0: no visible change, the ground for slices 1 to 5):

- **The backup format's versioning rule** (S4b-BL-72; docs/schemas README §1.1): every reader accepts
  `doorprints-backup/1` and `/2` and refuses a higher number as "made by a newer version of Doorprints; update
  Doorprints to import it" (`BackupFormat.READ_IDS` in `:shared` and on the server, `BACKUP_FORMATS_READ` on the web;
  the phone's `BackupReader` and `BackupValidation`, the server's `BackupService.validate`, whose `ImportReport` now
  echoes the file's format). Every writer still writes `/1`.
- **The server** (`app.doorprints.server.record`, Flyway `V6__records.sql`): one opaque `record` table
  (`type`, `id`, `payload jsonb`, `updated_at`, `deleted`, `sync_version`) and `GET /api/records?since=`,
  `PUT` and `DELETE /api/records/{type}/{id}` ([03](03-design.md) §9): last write wins under the one advisory lock,
  a payload that must be a JSON object of at most 65,536 bytes, at most 5,000 live rows per type (409 beyond), the
  rows deleted by `DELETE /api/data` and the tombstones purged with the others. The server never reads a payload.
- **The web** (S4b-BL-71, S4b-BL-66): `local-db.ts` upgrades **in steps by `oldVersion`** through a pure
  `upgradeLocalDb` (`DB_VERSION` 2: the `records` store keyed by `type` and `id` with a `type` index, and a `houseId`
  index on `photos` and on `visits`, read through the new `getAllByIndex`, so `photosOf` no longer reads every
  photo's bytes); an older tab closes its database on `versionchange` and shows "Doorprints was updated in another
  tab" with *Reload* (`app-banners.ts`, four languages). Records in `records.ts`, `LocalStore` (`recordsOf`,
  `saveRecord`, `deleteRecord`, `dirtyRecords`, `markRecordClean`, `putRecordFromServer`, the `record` cursor,
  `resetCursors`) and `SyncService` (pushed after the visits with the same reset check, pulled with the same two
  decisions as visits, a *records* progress phase).
- **The phone** (`:shared`): Room **4** (`records` table, `MIGRATION_3_4`, `4.json` pinned in `RoomSchemaTest`),
  `RecordEntity` and `RecordDao`, `RecordType<T>` and `RecordRules` in `shared/records` (the type and id rules,
  the payload cap, encode and decode with unknown keys ignored, an undecodable row skipped), `RecordDto` with
  `recordsSince` and `putRecord` in `ApiClient`, the `record` cursor in `Settings`, `CommonRepository`'s
  `observeRecords`, `saveRecord` and `deleteRecord` and its sync's push and pull of records.
- **Tests and docs**: [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-98, TC-I-42; docs/schemas README 1.10 (§1.1); [03](03-design.md)
  §6.1 and §9; CHANGELOG.

**Not built here:** no record type exists yet (brokers come with slice 1); the web's backup reader (S4b-BL-75) and a
Playwright check of the IndexedDB upgrade against the built site (S4b-BL-71's last item, `tools/live-ui`).

### 13.19 The house's cost, carpet area and location source, slice 1a of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-1-house-values-brokers`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.30
item 1 and 5.21, ADR-28; slice 1 in three steps, this the first):

- **The fields**, optional and additive on the house in all three stacks, so the backup stays `doorprints-backup/1`
  (docs/schemas README §1.1 and §3.1): `areaSqft` (1..100000), `locationSource` (`GPS`, `MAP`, `APPROX`) and the nested
  `cost` (`deposit`, `depositMonths`, `maintenance`, `maintenanceIncluded`, `brokerage`, `brokerageMonths`,
  `lockInMonths`, `noticeMonths`, `availableFrom`, `myOffer`, `agreedPrice`), written after `notes` and before
  `checklist` by every writer. Room **5** (`MIGRATION_4_5`, `5.json`; the cost embedded as `cost_*` columns), Flyway
  **`V7__house_values.sql`** (`area_sqft`, `location_source`, `cost jsonb`), IndexedDB unchanged (no store changed).
  `docs/schemas/backup-sample.json` carries them on houses 1 and 3; the web byte golden regenerated.
- **The arithmetic once**: `CostSummary` (`:shared`, `shared/model/HouseCost.kt`) and `costSummary`
  (`web/src/app/shared/house-cost.ts`) over one list of seven vectors: monthly cost (rent plus maintenance unless
  included), money to move in (deposit and brokerage, in rupees or months of rent, plus the first month), cost per sq
  ft from the agreed price when set, else the asked price.
- **Screens**: the form's *Carpet area* next to BHK, a **Cost** section (rent-only fields hidden for a sale, a date
  picker for *Available from*, the computed line under the fields) and the *Approximate location* switch; the maps
  draw an approximate house as a hollow ring in its status colour with a legend entry; Hunt mode never alerts at one
  (`HuntEngine`); Compare gains carpet area, monthly cost, money to move in, per sq ft, available from and the agreed
  price. The same on the website. Four languages (hi, ta, te *under review*), both themes; the form's screenshots
  re-recorded.
- **Copies and AI**: the houses table of the CSV ZIP and the XLSX gains the columns (the three computed ones too);
  HTML, PDF and Markdown get a *Cost* block and an *Approximate location* line; `AiHouse` and the server's
  `HouseDocuments` carry the area and the cost without *my offer*; the no-AI listing parser fills `areaSqft`.
- **Tests and docs**: [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-99; docs/schemas README 1.11; [03](03-design.md) §6.1, §9; the guide's
  houses page; CHANGELOG.

**Not built here:** brokers (1b) and rooms (1c); the price filters over the new numbers (**S4b-BL-84**); the viewing
questions' pre-fill (slice 3).

### 13.20 Brokers, slice 1b of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-1b-brokers`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.25 and 5.30, ADR-28;
the first use of the slice 0 records and the first `doorprints-backup/2` list):

- **The model**: a broker is a record of type `broker` (`name`, `phone`, `agency`, `feeTerms`, `notes`, `rating`;
  `Broker` in `:shared`, `shared/broker.ts` on the web) and a house carries `brokerId` (Room **6** `MIGRATION_5_6`,
  `6.json`; Flyway **`V8__house_broker.sql`**; the DTOs, the entities and the backup after `cost`). The contact name and
  phone stay on the house as copies of the broker's, so old apps, the exports and the AI redaction are untouched.
- **The rules, once in the repository of each app**: saving a house with a phone number links the broker with that
  number (compared on the last ten digits) or creates it, never for a blank number; saving a broker rewrites the
  copies on its houses; deleting one unlinks them and keeps their copies; contacts saved before were migrated once
  into one broker per distinct number, guarded by a flag so a person who unlinks one is not re-linked.
- **The backup** (docs/schemas README §3.4, 1.12): a `brokers` list after `photos`, `brokerId` on houses, `counts.brokers`;
  written as `/2` only when the copy has a broker, `/1` exactly as before otherwise, and neither in a copy made without
  contact details; import merges by id with the newest edit winning; the server maps the list to and from its `record`
  table. `backup-sample.json` is a `/2` document; the web byte golden regenerated.
- **Copies and search**: a `broker` column after `contactPhone` in the CSV and XLSX, `brokers.csv` and a *Brokers* sheet,
  a Broker row on each house page and a Brokers section in HTML, PDF and Markdown; search covers the broker's name,
  agency and fee terms. Brokers are never sent to AI.
- **Screens**: *Brokers* (Settings) with each broker's page (fields, *Call*, its houses, *Delete broker*), a *Broker*
  section in the house form, Compare's contact row showing the broker. Four languages (hi, ta, te *under review*),
  both themes; the form's and the new screen's screenshots.
- **Tests and docs**: [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-100; docs/schemas README 1.12; [03](03-design.md) §6.1, §9; the
  guide's houses and your-data pages; CHANGELOG.

**Not built here:** the duplicate-flat warning (**S4b-BL-85**; needs the floor, which arrives with rooms in 1c); rooms
(1c); a broker's rating in Compare; the Import screen's wording for brokers (its preview counts them only in the totals) and the
undo of a copy import, which leaves the brokers it added (**S4b-BL-86**); a *Matches <name>* hint while typing a phone.

### 13.21 Rooms, slice 1c of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-1c-rooms`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.6 and 5.30, ADR-28):

- **The field**: `rooms` on the house, at most 30, each `{id, type, name, lengthCm, widthCm, condition, notes, sort}`
  (docs/schemas README §3.5), after `cost` and before `brokerId` in every writer. Room **7** (`MIGRATION_6_7`, `7.json`, the
  list stored as JSON text like the checklist), Flyway **`V9__house_rooms.sql`**, IndexedDB unchanged. A writer now writes
  `doorprints-backup/2` when a copy holds a broker or a room; a copy made without contact details keeps its rooms.
- **Sizes and units**: stored in centimetres; a local *Length units* setting (*Feet* by default, *Metres*) shows feet and
  inches or metres and a room's area in sq ft or m² (`RoomSizes` in `:shared` and `room-sizes.ts`, one list of vectors).
- **Screens**: the form's **Rooms** section (type, name, length, width, condition, notes, *Add room*, *Delete*, the cap of 30, each
  room's area and the total), the *Length units* switch in Settings and on Your data, Compare's *Rooms* row. Four languages
  (hi, ta, te *under review*), both themes; one new screenshot of the section.
- **Copies, AI and search**: a `rooms` column, `rooms.csv`, a Rooms sheet and a Rooms table with a total on each house page; the
  AI documents carry names, sizes and condition, never a room's notes; search covers room names and notes.
- **Tests and docs**: [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-101; docs/schemas README 1.13 (§3.5); [03](03-design.md) §6.1, §9;
  [14](14-lead-backlog-and-handoff.md) §7 (the builders' models); the guide's houses page; CHANGELOG.

**Not built here:** reordering rooms (**S4b-BL-87**); the house's floor (needed by the duplicate-flat warning, S4b-BL-85);
photos linked to rooms (slice 5).

### 13.22 Criteria and ranking, slice 2 of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-2-criteria`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.4 and 5.30, ADR-28):

- **The model**: criteria are records of type `criterion` (the key is the id; `label`, `weight` 0..3, `mustHave`, `minScore`, `sort`,
  `archived`) and the rating share a record of type `preference` (`score.ratingShare`), so no table or migration; a built-in with no
  record uses the defaults and only what differs is stored; at most 40 criteria (docs/schemas README §3.6).
- **One scoring implementation per stack** (`HouseScore`/`Ranking` in `:shared`, `shared/scoring.ts`) over eight shared vectors: the
  weighted checklist, the blend with the rating, coverage, must-haves (failed or not yet checked) and the ranking order (no failed
  must-have, overall, coverage, price, newest). Every caller of the old score now passes the effective scoring: the house list,
  form and Compare, the Hunt notification, the readable copies and the PDF.
- **Screens**: *Criteria* (Settings on Android, a card on Your data on the web): weight, must-have with a minimum score, move up and
  down, archive and restore, add a criterion (at most 40), the rating share, reset to defaults. The house form lists the active criteria (a
  custom one by its name), shows how many that matter are scored and any missed must-have; the list marks a house that missed one and *best
  first* uses the ranking; Compare orders by it. Four languages (hi, ta, te *under review*), both themes.
- **Backup and copies**: `criteria` and `preferences` lists after `brokers` in `doorprints-backup/2` (a writer writes `/2` for a broker, a
  room, a criterion or a preference), merged by key on import, kept in a copy made without contact details; the server maps them to its
  `record` table. The scores table and the house pages use a custom criterion's name; a `criteria.csv` and a Criteria sheet; the
  ranking table follows the ranking. The AI and search are unchanged.
- **Tests and docs**: [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-102; docs/schemas README 1.14 (§3.6); [03](03-design.md) §9; the guide's houses
  and compare pages; CHANGELOG.

**Not built here:** the objective criteria from data (D-13); criteria synced across people (they sync like other records); a
per-search ranking scope toggle beyond the list's status filter.

### 13.23 Viewing questions, slice 3a of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-3a-questions`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.5 and 5.30, ADR-28):

- **The bank**: a `question` record per question (`text`, `category`, `appliesTo`, `defaultOn`, `sort`, `archived`; docs/schemas README §3.7), seeded once
  in the app's language from fourteen defaults with fixed ids (`docs/schemas/default-questions.json`, hi, ta and te *under review*), so two devices
  seed the same records; a deleted default stays deleted until *Reset to defaults*; at most 100 questions.
- **The answers**: nested in the house after `rooms` (`answers`, at most 60, §3.8): the question as asked, the answer, and Open, Answered or Skipped.
  Room **8** (`MIGRATION_7_8`, `8.json`), Flyway **`V10__house_answers.sql`**. *Add the usual questions* asks the defaults that fit the house and that
  it does not have yet, and fills deposit, maintenance, brokerage and lock-in from its cost (5.21); one implementation per stack over six shared vectors.
- **Screens**: the house form's **Questions to ask** (open first, answer, skip, remove, add the usual, add one from the bank or your own words, at most
  60) and **Questions** (Settings on Android, a card on Your data on the web): edit, category, rent or buy, ask by default, move, archive and bring
  back, delete, add (at most 100), reset. Four languages, both themes; one new screenshot.
- **Backup, copies, AI and search**: a `questions` list after `preferences` in `doorprints-backup/2` and `answers` in the house (a writer writes `/2`
  for either; both are kept in a copy made without contact details); a Questions table on each house page, `answers.csv` and an Answers sheet; the AI
  documents carry answered and still-open questions with contact details redacted; search covers answers.
- **Tests and docs**: [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-103; docs/schemas README 1.15 (§3.7 to §3.9); [03](03-design.md) §6.1, §9; the guide's houses page; CHANGELOG.

**Not built here:** the reminder card inside a viewing reminder (3b); questions grouped per viewing; sharing the bank between people beyond the
ordinary sync and the backup.

### 13.24 Viewings, slice 3b-1 of the data model, done in code

**Decided** (2026-09-30, [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.8, design table): a `viewing` record (`v_` id, `houseId`, `startsAt`, `durationMin`, `kind`, `status` PLANNED/DONE/CANCELLED, `remindMin`, `huntReminder`, `withWhom`, `notes`, `visitId`); MISSED is shown, not stored; no change to visits; the calendar file and Android's calendar insert; the reminders as pure rules plus one scheduler per platform. Built in two parts: **3b-1** (this section) then **3b-2** (Android alarms, iPhone notifications, the website's list). 3c, the Hunt reminder, follows.

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-3b1-viewings`; no Room, Flyway or IndexedDB migration):

- **The record**, on the three stacks: coercion on read, `missed`, `suggestedVisitFor`, the repository functions, and the cap of 5,000 (docs/schemas README §3.10).
- **Screens**: *Viewings* (Settings on Android, `/viewings` on the website; Upcoming, Missed?, Done, Cancelled; filters by date, kind and status; search; *It happened* and *Cancel* on a missed row), the form (plan, edit, cancel, delete, *Add to calendar*), the house card, and the second-viewing dialog with its re-check list. Four languages, both themes; two new screenshots.
- **Calendar file**: `docs/schemas/viewing-sample.ics` is produced byte for byte by both stacks (`ViewingIcs`, `viewingIcs`); Android inserts into the calendar, the website downloads the file.
- **Backup, copies, AI, server**: a `viewings` list in `doorprints-backup/2` (`withWhom` blanked without contact details); a Viewings section, `viewings.csv` and a sheet; AI lines (no `withWhom`, notes redacted on one line, at most ten); the server validates and stores the `viewing` record type.
- **Tests and docs**: [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-104; docs/schemas README 1.16 (§3.10, §3.11); [03](03-design.md) and [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md); the guide's houses page; CHANGELOG.
- **A flake fixed on the way**: specs that set the Tamil language left it in `localStorage`, so `compare-page.spec.ts` failed in about one run in three; the four specs now clear the store after each test.

**Not built here:** any reminder or notification (3b-2), the Hunt reminder (3c), the iPhone's `.ics` share, a backup importer on the website.

### 13.25 Viewing reminders, slice 3b-2 of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-3b2-reminders`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.8 design table, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-105; no backend change):

- **The rule**: `ViewingReminders` in `:shared` and TypeScript (PLANNED, `remindMin` above 0, `fireAt` after now, earliest 60), vectors R1..R6; the local setting `viewings.remind` (default on).
- **Android**: `ViewingReminderScheduler` (exact alarm when allowed, the early ten-minute window otherwise, WorkManager when no alarm can be set), rescheduled from the stored viewings (tombstones included) on start, on every change, on resume, on boot, time and time-zone change, app update and the exact-alarm permission change; channel `viewings`, private on the lock screen; actions *Open house*, *Directions*, *Questions*; the notification permission asked when the first reminder is saved; Settings > Viewings has the switch and the *Allow on-time reminders* note and button.
- **iPhone**: `IosViewingReminders` with calendar triggers (up to 60, identifiers `viewing-<id>`), rescheduled at start, on change and on resume. Compiled, not run.
- **Website**: a 60-second timer while the app is open, a browser notification or an in-page banner, the page switch *Notify me while Doorprints is open*, and a click handler in the service worker.

**Not built here:** the Hunt action (3c), scrolling to the questions from the *Questions* action, a screenshot of the new Settings section.

### 13.26 The Hunt mode reminder, slice 3c of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-3c-hunt-reminder`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.8 design table and 5.16, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-106; Android and iPhone only, no web or backend change):

- **The rule**: `HuntReminders` (`fireAt`, `upcoming`, `merged`, `showNow`), vectors H1..H7; local settings `hunt.remind` (default on) and `hunt.reminderMin` (5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 45 or 60; default 15).
- **Screens**: the viewing form's switch *Offer Hunt mode before this viewing* (phones), Settings > Hunt mode with the switch and the lead time; the iPhone Settings screenshots were re-recorded (8 files, none added).
- **Android**: the 3b-2 scheduler schedules both kinds (a merged pair is one alarm at the earlier time); channel `hunt_reminders`, private on the lock screen; *Start Hunt mode* (the service with fine location, otherwise the Map, which asks through its Hunt switch), *Dismiss*; the body opens the viewing; new deep links `OpenViewing` and `StartHunt`.
- **iPhone**: a `viewing-hunt-<id>` notification, a tap opens the viewing. Compiled, not run.

**Not built here:** TalkBack labels on the notification actions, the Map offering Hunt mode after an iPhone tap.

### 13.27 Hunting areas, my places and area notes, slice 4a of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-4a-areas-places`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.17, 5.22, 5.23 and the design table before 5.24, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-107; no migration):

- **Records**: `area` (at most 20, radius 200..2000 m, `enabled`), `place` (at most 10), `areanote` (on an area or a street, at most 200); derived rules for which houses a note reaches and for the distances to the places, vectors N1..N5 and D1..D3.
- **Screens**: *My areas*, *My places*, the notes list, the house page's *Area notes* and *Distances*, a Compare row per place; search finds a house by the notes that reach it.
- **Backup, copies, AI, server**: the lists `areas`, `places`, `areaNotes` in `doorprints-backup/2`; Area notes and Distances on the house page of the readable copies; AI lines `Area note:` and `Distance to <place>:`; the server validates and stores the three record types and computes the same lines.
- **Not built here:** the wake-up (slice 4b), the sortable distance column.

### 13.28 The area wake-up, slice 4b of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-4b-area-wakeup`, stacked on slice 4a; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.17, 5.18 and the design table before 5.24, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-108 and TC-M-35; Android only):

- **Rules**: `AreaCooldown` (6 h, inclusive; not while Hunt mode runs) and `AreaWakeup.geofencesFor` (enabled, live areas, at most 20), vectors C1..C7; local settings `areas.wakeup` (default off), a stamp per area and the one-time notice.
- **Geofences**: `AreaGeofenceManager` over a `GeofenceRegistrar` (ENTER only, no expiry), registered again at start, on changes, on resume, after boot and an update and on `GEOFENCE_NOT_AVAILABLE`; the receiver is not exported and the geofencing PendingIntent is the one mutable intent (commented in the manifest).
- **Notification**: channel `area_wakeup`, private on the lock screen, *Start Hunt mode* (the 3c path) and *Dismiss*; it never starts tracking by itself.
- **Permission model**: the rationale screen (equal *Continue* and *Not now*), precise location first, then "Allow all the time" (the system dialog on Android 10, the app's location page later), the card when it is lost. The switch is hidden without Play services and on the iPhone.

**Not built here:** the iPhone wake-up (S4b-BL-96, next), a listener for "location switched back on".

### 13.29 Photo tags and moving in, slice 5 of the data model, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-4c-slice-5-photo-tags-movein`, stacked on slice 4b; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.7 and 5.24 with the design table of slice 5, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-109; Room **9**, Flyway **V11** `house.move_in` and **V12** the photo meta columns; no IndexedDB version change):

- **Statuses**: `TAKEN` and `NOT_CHOSEN` on the three stacks (a file with either is `/2`); `HouseStatusRules` (at most one TAKEN; choosing it returns the previous one to SHORTLISTED; `closeTargets`) with the vectors M1..M3; *Mark the other houses Not chosen?* (**Mark them Not chosen** / **Keep them**) on saving a house as Taken. Colours: Taken amber `#8A5A00`, Not chosen grey `#5F6B66`, the same on both apps (Android took the website's; the dark pair followed in Wave D, §13.38).
- **Moving in**: the *Moving in* card of a Taken house (the house form on Android, the house page on the website): *Start moving in* adds the six items of `docs/schemas/default-movein.json` once, in the app's language; tick, edit, remove, *Add your own* (at most 30, text 1..200), the move-in date and notes (at most 2000); the **condition record** (the photos tagged Move-in, by room, with date and caption, and *Add a photo* with the tag already chosen); **Close this hunt** marks the `closeTargets` houses Not chosen and offers *Save a copy*. Vectors M4, M5.
- **Photo meta**: room, tags (the 15 fixed keys or the person's own, at most 10, vector M6) and caption, with `metaUpdatedAt` for last-write-wins; *Room, tags and caption* on each photo; `PUT /api/photos/{id}/meta` (an older edit changes nothing and answers the stored meta) and the meta in `PhotoDto`, so the photo change feed carries it on both syncs.
- **Copies, AI, search, server**: a Moving in section and the photos' room, tags and caption on the house page of the copies, the columns room, tags and caption in `photos.csv`; the AI lines `Moving in: <done> of <total> done` and `Moving in notes:` (redacted) on the three stacks; search over the move-in items and notes; the server validates `moveIn` and the meta and keeps them through a restore. `backup-sample.json` makes house 1 TAKEN with a move-in and photo 1 tagged (its `metaUpdatedAt` 1790000000000, in the past, so no test depends on today).
- **Decisions while building**: tags in the copies are the stored keys (not translated words); a *Shortlisted only* copy leaves Taken houses out; the Android list has no photos, so search there skips captions and tags, and the website's list does not search them either; the web copies label the move-in date *When*, Android's *Move-in date*. Each is in S4b-BL-99.

**Not built here:** archiving the hunt's houses (they are marked, not hidden); Compare and the website's and phones' own-key AI Plan still leave out only REJECTED (the server's planner skips NOT_CHOSEN too), S4b-BL-99. *(2026-10-01, v0.111: the Compare and Plan rule and the move-in date label are now the same everywhere, S4b-BL-99 a and d.)*

### 13.30 S4b-BL-96, the area wake-up on iPhone, done in code (compile only)

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-96-iphone-area-wakeup`, stacked on slice 5; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.17, 5.18, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-110):

- `IosAreaWakeup`: Core Location region monitoring of the enabled areas (at most 20, the platform's limit), registered again at start, on changes and on resume; the common `AreaCooldown` (6 hours) and `AreaWakeup.geofencesFor`; the setting shown where Core Location can monitor regions (`PlatformFeatures.areaWakeup`).
- The explanation screen names the iPhone and its two prompts ("While using", then "Always"); the notification permission is asked after "Always"; `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription` in `Info.plist`, checked by `shared-ios.yml`.
- On entering: "You're in <area>. Start Hunt mode?" as a notification; a tap opens the Map and offers Hunt mode (`DeepLink.StartHunt`; an iPhone app cannot start tracking from a notification). There is no *Dismiss* on iPhone, so the cooldown is stamped when the notification is posted. No `beginBackgroundTask`: the work done on a region event is only the cooldown check and the post.
- Tests: `AreaWakeupTest` (+6), `IosAreaWakeupTest` (3, iOS only, compiled), `theIphoneRationaleNamesIosAndItsTwoPrompts`, the launch self-check's `areaWakeup` gate.

**Not run:** real region monitoring and the prompts on an iPhone (TC-M-35, iPhone part).

### 13.31 S4b-BL-81 and S4b-BL-92a, copies, imports and the viewing's calendar file on iPhone, done in code (compile only)

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-81-iphone-copies-imports`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.2, 5.8, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-111):

- **Common code** (`:shared`): `Zip.kt` (a writer of stored entries and a reader of stored and deflated ones; SHA-256 and inflate come from the platform, CommonCrypto and zlib, `-lz` in `project.yml`), `BackupArchive.kt` (the archive checks of docs/schemas §6), `CopyWriter.kt` (every copy but the PDF), `ArchiveImports.kt` (the import with merge, copies and *Keep mine*).
- **iPhone** (`iosMain`): `IosArchive`, `IosCopies` and `IosSheets`: *Save a copy* through the share sheet and Files, *Import a backup* from Files and from a file another app opens in Doorprints (the backup's document type in `Info.plist`); *Add to calendar* shares the viewing's `.ics`. `PlatformFeatures.Ios`: copies and imports on, the PDF off; the launch self-check gains `copies` and `calendar`.
- Tests: `ArchiveTest` (14), `CommonArchiveInteropTest` (the common reader reads Android's files and the other way round), `ViewingCalendarFileTest`, `IosArchiveTest` (compiled only).

**Gaps** (S4b-BL-101): no PDF on iPhone; no undo of an import; the work runs only while the app's process lives and posts no result notification; the `.ics` from the iPhone stamps `DTSTAMP` with the share time; Android still writes with its own `:app` writer; the done message reads "Saved: <name>". On a device: TC-M-38.

### 13.32 The follow-ups that are code (S4b-BL-90, -92, -93, -94, -68), done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-94c-hunt-offer-and-followups`, head `6977299`; [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-112):

- **S4b-BL-94** (a) a deep link clears the tab's stack; (c) `DeepLink.OfferHunt`: after a reminder tap the Map shows the snackbar *Start Hunt mode?* (strings `map_hunt_offer`, `map_hunt_offer_start`), the iPhone included; (d) nothing left in code.
- **S4b-BL-93** (b) a reminder's *Questions* opens the house at its questions; (c) `ReminderRescheduleTest` asserts the start-up and on-resume reschedule; (f) the reminders keep their own asked flag, `viewings.notificationsAsked`.
- **S4b-BL-90** (a) the seeded questions are stamped 2000-01-01T00:00:00Z (the earliest `updatedAt` the server accepts) and written clean, so a fresh device never overwrites another's edits; *Reset to defaults* writes them dirty; (b) an import keeps to 100 questions and 40 criteria (backend and Android; `ImportCapsTest`).
- **S4b-BL-92** (c) copies use UTC on Kotlin as on the website (the cover says "Times shown for UTC +00:00"; the writer still takes any offset and the goldens keep IST, +330, to prove it); (d) a saved or imported viewing refreshes its house's AI document (backend); (e) and S4b-BL-90 (c): the undo of a copy import also removes the brokers, viewings, questions and criteria it created (not preferences, areas, places or notes).
- **S4b-BL-68**: with the app lock on, Hunt alerts are posted `VISIBILITY_SECRET`, so a locked screen shows nothing of them (`HuntAlertLockScreenTest`).
- Tests at the end of the batch: backend 498, web 1,195, Android in full and the iOS compile green.

**Left** to the manual list or the backlog: 94b TalkBack names (Wave D named the actions; the screen-reader run is TC-M-41), 93a and 94d on a device (TC-M-37), 93e the service worker's click and 90d the 5,000 cap test and the web *Your data* card (S4b-BL-103), 92b = S4b-BL-75 (§13.34), the native-reader items 90e, 92g, 93g, 94e (S4b-BL-109).

**What was done** (branch `feat/s4b-wave-b1-filters-rooms-brokers`; [11](11-feature-parity-and-export-spec.md) 5.6, 5.21, 5.25, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-113; Room **10**, Flyway **V13** `house.floor`):

No server bug was found.

### 13.38 Wave D, accessibility: the audit, the automated sweeps and the fixes, done in code

**What was done** (branch `feat/wave-d-accessibility`; [03](03-design.md) ADR-32, [06](06-test-plan.md) TC-U-WEB-A11Y-1..10, TC-U-117):

- **Website**: `shared/testing/a11y.ts` (role, name, label-in-name, focus and target-size helpers, with its own specs); `a11y-contrast.spec.ts` (about 55 colour pairs in both themes); `a11y-sweep.spec.ts` (44 pages in en, hi, ta and te). Eleven fixes: the Telugu room type BEDROOM was Korean text; the accessible names of Licence, a viewings row, the offline delete (hi, te), the photo details (ta, te) and the room delete (te) now contain their visible label; a floor out of range now **blocks the save** with a message and focus (it was silently dropped); *Areas* and *Places* focus the latitude field; the point picker's `aria-describedby`; *Add a question*'s `aria-controls`; the disclosure summaries' triangle; the Not chosen marker's opacity 0.6 to 0.75 (as Android); the sync badge 12 px to 0.75 rem. Web tests 1,591. S4b-BL-92f (focus in the website's dialogs) is now tested.
- **Android**: `A11yAudit`, `ScreensA11ySweepTest` (34 screens), `IndicScriptsTest`, `NotificationActionNamesTest`, `ContrastTest`; 14 findings, 9 fixed: *Save house here*'s name; the stars, the Tamil visits row and the criteria share at 200 % text; `house_loading`; the dark Taken `#F2C265` and Not chosen `#B4BEB9` (as the website); the notification actions name what they do (*Directions to the house* and the like; `notif_copy_open`, `notif_copy_share`, `notif_task_stop`); a dark Viewings shot. Android 540 `:app`, 165 `:ui`, 609 `:shared` tests green.
- **Switch contrast** (on `feat/wave-e-seo`, commit `7dcf4db`): the unchecked thumb of every switch was 2.93:1 on its track; it now takes `onSurfaceVariant`, `#5F6B67` (4.48:1) and dark `#A7B3AE` (5.87:1), through `BrandSwitchPalette` / `brandSwitchColors` in `Theme.kt`, used by `SwitchRow` and the Map's Hunt switch; 26 screenshots changed. The website has no such switch.

**Not fixed** (S4b-BL-110): the stars at 4.44:1 (fixed 2026-10-01: `#966000`, 4.52:1 or more); Compose's fades under the iPhone's Reduce Motion (2026-10-01: Compose Multiplatform 1.12.1 already honours it, a device check left); focus after dialogs on Android (manual); Android's `enableAccessibilityChecks` (it needs a new test dependency, not added). **Manual** (TC-M-41, TC-M-42): TalkBack, VoiceOver, NVDA, 200 % text and 360 px on a device, the focus ring, forced colours, the map by keyboard, the native dialog's focus trap, MapLibre and reduced motion, the Indic fonts on OEM phones, Switch Access.

### 13.39 Wave E, the website's SEO, done in code

- `seo.spec.ts`; web tests 1,624.

**Not feasible here** (S4b-BL-102): per-language `hreflang` (the app has one address for four languages), prerendering, a Lighthouse check in CI. **Owner** (TC-M-40): Search Console with the sitemap, a share-preview check, a native reading of the hi/ta/te about page, landing text and title.
